Pressure conjugacy:

\[\frac{p}{k_{B}T} = \frac{\partial\ln W}{\partial V}.\]

Chemical conjugacy:

\[- \frac{\mu_{i}}{k_{B}T} = \frac{\partial\ln W}{\partial N_{i}}.\]

Generalized conjugacy:

\[- \frac{Y_{\alpha}}{k_{B}T} = \frac{\partial\ln W}{\partial X_{\alpha}}.\]

Possibility:

\[P = \text{compatible~enclosing~possibility}\text{.}\]

Retention:

\[R = C^{out}\lambda\]

Work-like energetic read:

\[k_{B}T\text{ }dR \longleftrightarrow Y_{\alpha}\text{ }dX_{\alpha}.\]

Local asymmetry:

\[A = P - R.\]

Stall:

\[P = R\text{\:\,} \Longleftrightarrow \text{\:\,}A = 0.\]

Fixed-\(T,p\) Gibbs read:

\[\Delta S + \Delta P = - \frac{\Delta g}{k_{B}T}.\]

Restricted thermal possibility read:

\[\Delta P^{th} = - \frac{\Delta H}{k_{B}T}.\]

First Law: \(\text{energetic~closure~within~a~specified~thermodynamic~boundary}\text{.}\)

R²D Law: \(\text{recurrence-weighted~relation~between~adjacent-boundary~reads}\text{.}\)

Second Law: \(\text{nonreciprocal~realization} \leftrightarrow \text{enclosing~background~production}\text{.}\)

7.29 What Has Been Established and What Has Been Mapped

The logical status should remain explicit. Part I establishes the dimensionless count architecture:

\[{S = \ln W, }{R = C^{out}\lambda, }{A = P - R,}\]

the recurrence-weighted R²D law, and the decomposition

\[C = C^{out} + C^{\Phi}.\]

Chapter 1 establishes, within its stated chemical domain,

\[\Delta S + \Delta P = - \frac{\Delta g}{k_{B}T}.\]

Chapter 2 establishes temperature as the energetic scale of thermal compatibility and retains the enthalpy mapping only for the specified thermal-reservoir contribution.

Chapter 6 maps nonreciprocal realization onto thermodynamic entropy generation and enclosing background.

This chapter adds conventional equilibrium thermodynamics as a physical coordinate system:

\[dU = T\text{ }d\Sigma - p\text{ }dV + \sum_{i}\mu_{i}\text{ }dN_{i} + \sum_{\alpha}Y_{\alpha}\text{ }dX_{\alpha}.\]

It identifies the resulting conjugate variables as energetic slopes of logarithmic count. It does not derive the First Law from combinatorics. It does not derive universal conservation of energy. It does not define a universally conserved energetic substance. Instead, it locates the First Law precisely:

\[\text{energetic~conservation~is~a~closure~property~of~an~appropriate~thermodynamic~read}\]

7.30 The Thermodynamic Structure That Emerges

The thermodynamic architecture can now be stated without elevating energy to primitive status. A boundary defines a count domain. Multiplicity defines possible structure:

\[S = \ln W.\]

A thermodynamic boundary can assign dimensional energetic coordinates to that structure. The slopes of the resulting state relation define the familiar conjugates:

\[T, p, \mu, Y,\ldots\]

Legendre transformations produce boundary-adapted state coordinates:

\[U, H, F, G.\]

Enclosing compatibility supplies possibility. The retaining/output component of enclosing constraint supplies local retention:

\[R = C^{out}\lambda.\]

Residual local direction is

\[A = P - R.\]

The R²D law relates recurrence-weighted reads of the same recursive occurrence across adjacent boundaries. The First Law requires energetic closure when a valid thermodynamic energetic boundary closes. The Second Law determines whether realization is reciprocal or produces enclosing background. Thus:

\[{R^{2}\text{D~Law} = \text{recursive~readability} }{\text{First~Law} = \text{local~energetic~closure} }{\text{Second~Law} = \text{realization~reciprocity~and~background}\text{.}}\]

None requires a universally conserved primitive energy object. The thermodynamic regime is instead the regime in which recursive count, energetic state, conjugate constraint, recurrence, work, heat, and background are sufficiently jointly readable to support a closed thermodynamic description.

Energy has therefore not replaced recursive count. Energy is the boundary-specific physical coordinate through which recursive count becomes thermodynamically readable.

Chapter 8 — Chemical Activity and the H-Theorem: Direction Before Mechanism

8.1 From Gibbs Direction to Chemical State

Chapter 1 established the exact thermodynamic mapping

\[\Delta G^{pre} = \Delta S + \Delta P = - \frac{\Delta_{r}G}{RT}\]

The complete pre-realization direction of a chemical transition is therefore already determined before any particular molecular reaction event occurs. The question now becomes more specific. What changes when the chemical environment changes while the local reaction architecture remains the same?

Conventional thermodynamics answers through chemical potential and activity. For species \(j\),

\[\mu_{j} = \mu_{j}^{\circ} + RT\ln a_{j}.\]

For a chemical reaction with stoichiometric coefficients \(\nu_{j}\),

\[\Delta_{r}G = \sum_{j}\nu_{j}\mu_{j}.\]

Therefore,

\[\Delta_{r}G = \Delta_{r}G^{\circ} + RT\ln Q,\]

where

\[Q = \prod_{j}a_{j}^{\nu_{j}}.\]

The chemical state therefore contains a directional relation before a particular reaction event is selected. Activity belongs to the chemical state, while collision-based realization enters later.

This makes chemical activity unusually important for R²D. It provides an experimentally familiar thermodynamic coordinate of enclosing directional possibility.

8.2 Standard-State Direction

At the standard thermodynamic condition,

\[Q^{\circ} = 1.\]

Chapter 1 established

\[\Delta G^{pre, \circ} = \Delta S + \Delta P^{\circ}\]

and

\[\Delta G^{pre, \circ} = - \frac{\Delta_{r}G^{\circ}}{RT}.\]

Therefore,

\[\Delta S + \Delta P^{\circ} = - \frac{\Delta_{r}G^{\circ}}{RT}.\]

The standard-state Gibbs energy therefore does not read native multiplicity alone. It reads the complete boundary-defined direction under the chosen standard enclosing condition. In particular,

\[\Delta P^{\circ}\]

need not vanish. Thus, the standard state is not the absence of an environment. It is a chosen enclosing reference condition.

8.3 Chemical Activity Changes Enclosing Possibility

Now compare the same local state architecture under standard and nonstandard thermodynamic conditions. At arbitrary conditions,

\[\Delta G^{pre} = \Delta S + \Delta P.\]

At the standard condition,

\[\Delta G^{pre, \circ} = \Delta S + \Delta P^{\circ}.\]

Subtracting,

\[\Delta G^{pre} - \Delta G^{pre, \circ} = \Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ}.\]

Using

\[\Delta G^{pre} = - \frac{\Delta_{r}G}{RT}\]

and

\[\Delta G^{pre, \circ} = - \frac{\Delta_{r}G^{\circ}}{RT},\]

gives

\[\Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ} = - \frac{\Delta_{r}G - \Delta_{r}G^{\circ}}{RT}.\]

But

\[\Delta_{r}G - \Delta_{r}G^{\circ} = RT\ln Q.\]

Therefore,

\[\Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ} = - \ln Q.\]

This is not an analogy. Reaction activity is the change in enclosing possibility.

8.4 Species Activity Coordinates

Since

\[\ln Q = \sum_{j}\nu_{j}\ln a_{j},\]

the activity theorem becomes

\[\Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ} = - \sum_{j}\nu_{j}\ln a_{j}.\]

Define the dimensionless activity coordinate

\[\phi_{j}^{(a)} = - \ln a_{j}.\]

Then

\[\Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ} = \sum_{j}\nu_{j}\phi_{j}^{(a)}.\]

Conventional chemical potential gives

\[\mu_{j} - \mu_{j}^{\circ} = RT\ln a_{j}.\]

Therefore,

\[\mu_{j} - \mu_{j}^{\circ} = - RT\phi_{j}^{(a)}.\]

At the reaction level,

\[\sum_{j}\nu_{j}\left( \mu_{j} - \mu_{j}^{\circ} \right) = RT\ln Q.\]

Using the R²D activity theorem,

\[\Delta_{r}G - \Delta_{r}G^{\circ} = - RT\left( \Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ} \right)\]

Thus, the activity-dependent part of chemical Gibbs direction is exactly the energetic thermodynamic read of the change in enclosing possibility.

8.5 What Chemical Potential Reads

Chemical potential should therefore be handled carefully. The full chemical potential is

\[\mu_{j} = \mu_{j}^{\circ} + RT\ln a_{j}.\]

The activity-dependent component has a direct dimensionless coordinate:

\[\mu_{j} - \mu_{j}^{\circ} = - RT\phi_{j}^{(a)}.\]

But the full standard contribution

\[\mu_{j}^{\circ}\]

should not yet be assigned solely to \(S\), solely to \(P^{\circ}\), or to another individual primitive quantity. At the reaction level, what is established is

\[- \frac{\Delta_{r}G^{\circ}}{RT} = \Delta S + \Delta P^{\circ}\]

Thus, standard chemical direction already contains both native and enclosing contributions. The safe interpretation is therefore:

\[\mu_{j} = \text{species-level~thermodynamic~coordinate~contributing~to~Gibbs~direction}\text{,}\]

while

\[RT\ln a_{j} = \text{activity-dependent~shift~in~that~coordinate}\text{.}\]

R²D has an exact mapping of the stoichiometric activity combination onto enclosing possibility. It does not yet require a unique primitive decomposition of every individual standard chemical potential.

8.6 Why Activity Is an Enclosing Relation

This result gives chemical activity a natural causal interpretation. A local molecular reaction does not determine its own activity. Activity depends on the state in which the species is embedded.

The same nominal species can participate in different chemical environments and therefore possess different thermodynamic activities. The attached notes emphasize that activity is valuable precisely because local amount alone is insufficient: surrounding chemical conditions matter.

Within R²D this becomes exact at the reaction level:

\[- \ln Q = \Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ}.\]

Changing activity therefore changes enclosing compatibility while the native local reaction architecture can remain fixed. The causal sequence is

\[\text{enclosing~chemical~state} \longrightarrow \text{activity~relation} \longrightarrow \text{reaction~direction}\text{.}\]

The particular molecular reaction event comes afterward as realization.

8.7 Reaction Direction Can Reverse Without Changing the Mechanism

Chapter 1 established

\[\frac{\Theta_{k}}{\Theta_{i}} = \frac{K_{eq}}{Q}.\]

Therefore,

\[{Q < K_{eq}\text{\:\,} \Longleftrightarrow \text{\:\,}\Theta_{k} > \Theta_{i}\text{\:\,} \Longleftrightarrow \text{\:\,}\Delta_{r}G < 0, }{Q = K_{eq}\text{\:\,} \Longleftrightarrow \text{\:\,}\Theta_{k} = \Theta_{i}\text{\:\,} \Longleftrightarrow \text{\:\,}\Delta_{r}G = 0, }\]and

\[Q > K_{eq}\text{\:\,} \Longleftrightarrow \text{\:\,}\Theta_{k} < \Theta_{i}\text{\:\,} \Longleftrightarrow \text{\:\,}\Delta_{r}G > 0.\]

The same chemical reaction mechanism can therefore be supported in opposite directions simply by changing the enclosing chemical state.

Nothing requires the local reaction mechanism or state-transition architecture to be replaced. This is the chemical analogue of the enclosing-reversal theorem of Part I. The local mechanism may remain the same. The enclosing compatibility relation changes. The direction reverses.

Thus, the same local mechanism with different enclosing compatibility can have different chemical directions. This is difficult to reconcile with the claim that the local mechanism itself is the primitive source of direction.

8.8 The H-Theorem Returns

The H-theorem now reappears in a more revealing role. Chapter 5 treated it as the historical statistical-mechanical problem of irreversible direction. The conventional kinetic narrative is

\[molecular collisions \longrightarrow distribution change \longrightarrow relaxation.\]

But the H-theorem requires statistical organization of the incoming molecular ensemble that is not supplied by the reversible collision law itself. The attached notes therefore rewrite the causal architecture as

\[\text{enclosing~statistical~asymmetry} \longrightarrow \text{directional~statistical~organization} \longrightarrow \text{collision-level~realization}\]

Chemical activity poses the same causal problem in a thermodynamic form. The conventional chemical narrative is often

\[\text{molecular~encounters} \longrightarrow \text{reaction} \longrightarrow \text{chemical~direction}.\]

But reaction direction is already specified by

\[\Delta_{r}G = \Delta_{r}G^{\circ} + RT\ln Q.\]

And R²D now proves

\[- \ln Q = \Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ}.\]

Therefore,

\[\text{enclosing~chemical~asymmetry} \longrightarrow \text{chemical~direction} \longrightarrow \text{reaction-level~realization}\text{.}\]

The H-theorem and chemical activity are therefore two instances of the same structural question: Does the local stochastic event create direction, or realize direction already defined by a larger statistical relation?

8.9 They Are the Same Problem, Not the Same Equation

This distinction should be explicit. The H-theorem's statistical assumption and chemical activity are not mathematically identical quantities. R²D has not proved

\[\text{molecular~chaos} = P.\]

Nor has it proved that the H-theorem's incoming-ensemble condition is numerically identical to

\[- \ln Q.\]

The connection is causal and structural. For the H-theorem, statistical organization precedes collision realization. For chemical activity, chemical-state organization precedes reaction realization.

The chemical case is stronger within the present development because the mapping

\[\Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ} = - \ln Q\]

is exact. The H-theorem supplies the historical template. Chemical activity supplies the direct thermodynamic realization of the R²D enclosing relation.

8.10 Molecular Chaos and Chemical Activity Occupy Parallel Logical Positions

The parallel can be stated more sharply. In Boltzmann kinetics, the local collision law does not itself specify the statistical organization of all incoming collision partners. A separate statistical condition is required.

In chemistry, the molecular reaction event does not itself specify the activities of the reactants and products. Those are state relations supplied by the chemical environment. Thus,

Statistical mechanics Chemical thermodynamics
Incoming ensemble organization Chemical activity state
Collision event Reaction event
Distributional realization Composition realization
Statistical direction Gibbs reaction direction
Local dynamics realize Local chemistry realizes

The common architecture is

\[\text{state-level~statistical~relation} \longrightarrow \text{local~stochastic~realization}\text{.}\]

The local event remains necessary. It is not thereby the source of the state-level relation.

8.11 Equilibrium Is the Decisive Experiment

Equilibrium makes the separation particularly clear. At chemical equilibrium,

\[Q = K_{eq},\]so

\[\Delta_{r}G = 0.\]Equivalently,

\[\Theta_{k} = \Theta_{i}.\]

Yet forward and reverse molecular reactions continue. The local mechanism remains. Collision events remain. Forward and reverse molecular realizations continue. What disappears is the net directional chemical asymmetry.

The same logic appears in the H-theorem. At equilibrium, molecular collisions continue. What disappears is the systematic distributional direction toward another macrostate.

Thus, equilibrium in both cases separates realization mechanism from directional organization.

8.12 Direction Vanishes Before Mechanism Does

The distinction can be made even more compactly. For chemistry,

\[\Delta_{r}G \rightarrow 0\]

while

\[\text{reaction~events} \neq 0.\]

For Boltzmann kinetics,

\[\text{net~distributional~relaxation} \rightarrow 0\]

while

\[\text{collisions} \neq 0.\]

Thus, in both cases direction can vanish while the mechanism remains active. This is strong evidence that mechanism and direction are different causal objects.

The mechanism answers: \(\text{How~is~the~state~relation~locally~realized?}\)

The enclosing thermodynamic or statistical state answers: \(\text{Which~direction~is~supported?}\)

8.13 Mass Action Is a Realization Law

The same distinction carries into chemical kinetics. For a reaction such as

\[A + B \rightleftharpoons C + D,\]

mass-action kinetics may be written schematically as

\[r_{+} \propto a_{A}a_{B},\]

and

\[r_{-} \propto a_{C}a_{D}.\]

These relations are extraordinarily successful. R²D need not challenge them. The question is what causal role they play.

A factorized rate law can accurately describe how frequently local chemical realizations occur without proving that those molecular encounters generate the Gibbs direction being realized.

Mass action may count the frequency with which a chemical relation is locally realized while remaining silent about the causal origin of that direction. Thus, chemical thermodynamics determines direction; chemical kinetics determines realization frequency. This is the chemical version of the H-theorem distinction.

8.14 Activity Does Not Require Molecular Chaos

One qualification matters. Chemical activity itself does not assume molecular chaos. The thermodynamic relation

\[\mu_{j} = \mu_{j}^{\circ} + RT\ln a_{j}\]

is already defined at the state level. The molecular-chaos-like assumption appears only when one attempts to represent that state-level relation through a factorized collision or encounter model. Thus, the logical sequence is

\[\text{activity~relation} \rightarrow \text{chemical~direction} \rightarrow \text{collision-based~kinetic~realization}\text{.}\]

Not

\[\text{molecular~chaos} \rightarrow \text{activity}.\]

This prevents the H-theorem analogy from becoming too strong. The two theories expose the same causal structure at different levels.

8.15 Probability, Activity, and Rate Are Three Different Reads

Probability is a normalized read of count. Activity is a thermodynamic coordinate of chemical state. Physical rate is a read of completed recurrence relative to a physical time coordinate. These should not be conflated.

Schematically,

\[\Theta \longrightarrow \text{direction}\]

is the possible-count relation.

\[a_{j},\text{\:\,}Q,\text{\:\,}\Delta_{r}G\]

are thermodynamic reads of that chemical directional structure.

\[r_{+},\text{\:\,}r_{-},\text{\:\,}k_{+},\text{\:\,}k_{-}\]

describe kinetic realization. The conceptual error occurs only when the last layer is promoted to the causal source of the first. The reaction events are real. The rates are real. The molecular encounters are real. What R²D rejects is the inference:

\[\text{local~realization~mechanism} \Rightarrow \text{primitive~source~of~direction}.\]

8.16 Chemical Activity Is Not an EP Error

Chemical activity itself is not an error. It is a successful thermodynamic coordinate. Indeed, R²D gives it a direct count interpretation:

\[- \ln Q = \Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ}.\]

Likewise, mass-action kinetics is not an error merely because it is written in terms of molecular encounters. The EP error occurs when a readable local consequence is assigned the primitive causal role of an omitted enclosing relation.

That is the same error exposed historically by the H-theorem.

8.17 The Unified H-Theorem—Activity Structure

The common architecture can now be written in one form.

Boltzmann

\[\text{enclosing~statistical~organization} \longrightarrow \text{directional~distribution} \longrightarrow \text{collision~realization}.\]

Chemistry

\[\text{enclosing~chemical~compatibility} \longrightarrow \text{Gibbs~direction} \longrightarrow \text{reaction~realization}\text{.}\]

The conventional bottom-up interpretation reverses the arrows:

\[\text{collisions} \rightarrow \text{statistical~direction},\]

and

\[\text{molecular~encounters} \rightarrow \text{chemical~direction}.\]

R²D instead identifies one common relation. Enclosing asymmetry defines direction while subordinate stochastic events realize it.

8.18 Chemical Activity Provides the Stronger Test

Chemical activity gives this causal architecture an advantage over the H-theorem alone. The H-theorem exposes the need for a statistical condition not supplied by the local reversible collision law.

Chemical thermodynamics goes further. The enclosing contribution is directly controllable and measurable. Change the activities. Change \(Q\).

Then

\[\Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ} = - \ln Q\]

changes. The reaction direction can weaken, vanish, or reverse. The molecular mechanism need not change. Thus, chemical activity provides a direct experimental manifestation of the same enclosing-to-local architecture that the H-theorem exposes conceptually.

8.19 What Has Been Established

The chemical mapping can now be summarized. The complete reaction direction is

\[\Delta S + \Delta P = - \frac{\Delta_{r}G}{RT}.\]

The standard-state direction is

\[\Delta S + \Delta P^{\circ} = - \frac{\Delta_{r}G^{\circ}}{RT}.\]

Their difference is

\[\Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ} = - \ln Q.\]

Equivalently,

\[\Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ} = - \sum_{j}\nu_{j}\ln a_{j}.\]

Thus, the activity-dependent contribution to reaction direction is an exact thermodynamic read of changing enclosing possibility. The H-theorem presents the same causal problem in kinetic language:

\[\text{statistical~organization} \rightarrow \text{collision~realization}\text{.}\]

Chemical activity presents it in thermodynamic language:

\[\text{chemical-state~organization} \rightarrow \text{reaction~realization}\text{.}\]

They are not the same equation. They expose the same inversion of causal agency. That gives a natural transition to the next chapter.

Once chemical direction is already defined by Gibbs free energy and activity, what determines the rate at which that direction is realized?

That is the problem of chemical kinetics, mass action, detailed balance, and coupled reactions.

Chapter 9 — Chemical Kinetics: Direction, Flux, and Realization

9.1 The Question Changes from Direction to Realization

The preceding chapters have already determined chemical direction. For a reaction under the fixed-\(T,p\) thermodynamic conditions of the Gibbs bridge,

\[\Delta G^{pre} = \Delta S + \Delta P = - \frac{\Delta_{r}G}{RT}.\]

Chemical activity changes that direction through

\[\Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ} = - \ln Q.\]

Thus, before any particular molecular reaction event is selected, the chemical state already possesses a directional relation. Chemical kinetics therefore begins with a different question.

Not: What determines reaction direction?

But: How frequently is an already-defined chemical direction locally realized?

Mass-action kinetics can accurately describe how often a chemical relation is realized without thereby establishing that molecular encounters are the source of the direction being realized. The separation is

\[{\text{t}\text{hermodynamics} \rightarrow \text{direction} }{\text{kinetics} \rightarrow \text{realization}\text{.} }\]

The two must subsequently be consistent. They are not the same relation.

9.2 Occurrence and Closure Recurrence Are Prior to Physical Rate

Part I already defines a primitive count of realization. Let

\[\nu_{n,i}\]

be the number of realized occurrences associated with macrostate \(i\) within a specified finite realization domain. Its logarithmic coordinate is

\[\tau_{n,i} = \ln\nu_{n,i}.\]For two macrostates,

\[\Delta\tau_{n,i \rightarrow k} = \ln\left( \frac{\nu_{n,k}}{\nu_{n,i}} \right).\]

But Part I is explicit about what this does not mean. The realization domain defining \(\nu\) is not assumed to be a physical time interval. Therefore,

\[\tau\]is not physical time, and

\[\Delta\tau\]is not a rate.

Chemical kinetics therefore requires a physical readout of occurrence count. It does not permit physical rate to be inserted into Part I retroactively.

Chemical kinetics also requires a count of completed local closures. Let N_Cₙ denote the number of completed closures of a representative local reaction cycle in a specified recurrence-comparison domain. This is not the same as the state occurrence count νₙ,ᵢ and it is not the occupancy cₙ,ᵢ.

\[N_{C,n} \neq \nu_{n,i} \neq c_{n,i}.\]

When a physical time coordinate is supplied, closure recurrence acquires the rate read rₙ=N_Cₙ/Δt. The logarithmic occurrence coordinate τ=lnν remains a relative occurrence statistic; it is not replaced by physical rate.

9.3 From Occurrence Count to Measured Flux

Suppose a reversible chemical transformation is observed during a physical interval

\[\Delta t.\]Let

\[N_{+}\]

be the number of forward reaction events observed during that interval and

\[N_{-}\]

the number of reverse events. The corresponding one-way reaction fluxes are

\[J_{+} = \frac{N_{+}}{\Delta t}\]and

\[J_{-} = \frac{N_{-}}{\Delta t}.\]

Absolute flux therefore requires a physical time coordinate. But the ratio does not:

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} = \frac{N_{+}/\Delta t}{N_{-}/\Delta t}.\]Hence,

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} = \frac{N_{+}}{N_{-}}.\]Taking logarithms,

\[\ln\left( \frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} \right) = \ln\left( \frac{N_{+}}{N_{-}} \right).\]

This is useful for R²D because the common physical time scale cancels. Thus, relative directional kinetics can be connected to primitive occurrence count before a primitive derivation of physical time has been completed.

Absolute rate still requires a physical readout scale. The forward-to-reverse realization ratio does not.

9.4 Mass Action Supplies the Chemical Realization Law

Consider an elementary reversible reaction

\[\sum_{j}\alpha_{j}X_{j} \rightleftharpoons \sum_{j}\beta_{j}X_{j}.\]

For thermodynamically consistent mass-action kinetics, the one-way fluxes may be written

\[J_{+} = k_{+}\prod_{j}a_{j}^{\alpha_{j}},\]

and

\[J_{-} = k_{-}\prod_{j}a_{j}^{\beta_{j}}.\]

The reaction quotient is

\[Q = \frac{\prod_{j}a_{j}^{\beta_{j}}}{\prod_{j}a_{j}^{\alpha_{j}}}.\]Therefore,

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} = \frac{k_{+}}{k_{-}}\frac{1}{Q}.\]At equilibrium,

\[J_{+} = J_{-},\]and

\[Q = K_{eq}.\]

Thus, for this reversible elementary mass-action realization,

\[\frac{k_{+}}{k_{-}} = K_{eq}.\]Consequently,

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} = \frac{K_{eq}}{Q}.\]

This relation applies to a thermodynamically consistent reversible elementary step, or to a coarse-grained model constructed so that the same local detailed-balance relation is preserved. It should not be asserted for arbitrary phenomenological rate constants.

9.5 The Kinetic Ratio Is the Gibbs Count Ratio

Chapter 1 established the exact thermodynamic mapping

\[\frac{\Theta_{n + 1 \mid n,k}}{\Theta_{n + 1 \mid n,i}} = \frac{K_{eq}}{Q}.\]

The mass-action result therefore gives

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} = \frac{\Theta_{n + 1 \mid n,k}}{\Theta_{n + 1 \mid n,i}}.\]And because

\[\ln\left( \frac{\Theta_{k}}{\Theta_{i}} \right) = - \frac{\Delta_{r}G}{RT},\]we obtain

\[\ln\left( \frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} \right) = - \frac{\Delta_{r}G}{RT}.\]Equivalently,

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} = \exp\left( - \frac{\Delta_{r}G}{RT} \right).\]For one molecular reaction event,

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} = \exp\left( - \frac{\Delta G_{sys}}{k_{B}T} \right).\]

The same directional relation now has three readings:

\[\frac{\Theta_{k}}{\Theta_{i}} = \frac{K_{eq}}{Q} = \frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}}.\]

The first is compatible enclosing count. The second is chemical thermodynamics. The third is kinetic realization.

9.6 Direction and Rate Are Therefore Different Quantities

The previous equation is a ratio of forward and reverse realization. It does not determine how large either rate is. For example, the pair

\[J_{+} = 100, J_{-} = 10\]

and the pair

\[J_{+} = 10^{6}, J_{-} = 10^{5}\]

have the same directional ratio:

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} = 10.\]

But their absolute kinetic scales differ enormously. Thus, chemical kinetics contains at least two distinguishable questions.

The first is directional:

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} = e^{- \Delta_{r}G/RT}.\]

The second is kinetic: \(\text{How~large~are~}J_{+}\text{~and~}J_{-}\text{~individually?}\)

The first is fixed by thermodynamic direction. The second depends on the local realization geometry and on the enclosing compatibility and constraint relations under which that geometry is realized. Mechanism therefore matters, but it does not set an absolute recurrence scale independently of the thermodynamic boundary.

\[\text{thermodynamic~asymmetry} \rightarrow \text{forward/reverse~bias}.\]

while

\[local realization geometry + enclosing relation \rightarrow recurrence frequency\]

A mechanism can change recurrence without changing thermodynamic direction; conversely, enclosing temperature, pressure, activity, or mechanical constraint can change recurrence while the local mechanism and native multiplicity classification remain available.

Principle — Enclosing Organization of Recurrence

Native multiplicity defines the possible architecture of local realization but does not fix the number of completed local recurrences. At a nonterminal boundary, closure is realized within an enclosing compatibility and constraint relation. Changes in that enclosing relation may therefore change recurrence while leaving the native multiplicity landscape unchanged:

\[\Delta W_{n} = 0 \nRightarrow \Delta N_{C,n} = 0.\]

9.7 Catalysis Makes the Distinction Visible

This immediately explains the logical role of catalysis. A catalyst alters local realization geometry and recurrence, not a traversed pathway. dIt may change the magnitudes of

\[k_{+}\]and

\[k_{-}.\]

But thermodynamic consistency requires their ratio to preserve the equilibrium relation:

\[\frac{k_{+}}{k_{-}} = K_{eq}\]

for the corresponding elementary reversible step. Thus, the catalyst may strongly alter

\[J_{+}\]and

\[J_{-},\]while leaving

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}}\]

at a specified chemical state thermodynamically constrained.

This provides a particularly clear example of the difference between mechanism and direction. The catalyst changes how rapidly the relation is realized. It does not create the equilibrium relation.

9.8 Equilibrium Does Not Mean No Kinetics

At equilibrium,

\[Q = K_{eq}.\]Therefore,

\[\Delta_{r}G = 0.\]

The forward-to-reverse flux relation becomes

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} = 1.\]Hence,

\[J_{+} = J_{-}.\]But generally,

\[J_{+} \neq 0\]and

\[J_{-} \neq 0.\]

Forward and reverse reaction occurrences remain active. Forward reactions continue. Reverse reactions continue. What vanishes is the directional imbalance between them.

This as the decisive conceptual experiment: the molecular mechanism remains while net chemical direction disappears. Thus,

\[\text{equilibrium} \neq \text{absence~of~occurrence}\text{.}\]Instead,

\[\text{equilibrium} = \text{reciprocal~forward/reverse~realization}\text{.}\]

9.9 Detailed Balance Is a Realization Symmetry

For the elementary reversible step considered above, equilibrium gives

\[J_{+} = J_{-}.\]

This is the familiar local detailed-balance condition. R²D gives it a particularly simple interpretation. At equilibrium,

\[\Theta_{k} = \Theta_{i}.\]

Therefore,

\[\Delta G^{pre} = 0.\]

The enclosing boundary supplies no net directional preference between the two chemical alternatives. The local kinetic realization then satisfies

\[J_{+} = J_{-}.\]Thus:

\[\text{equal~enclosing~directional~support} \longleftrightarrow \text{equal~opposed~kinetic~realization}\text{.}\]

The mechanism has not disappeared. Its opposed realizations have become reciprocal. This is the chemical analogue of the distinction developed in the H-theorem: mechanism persists; direction disappears.

9.10 Detailed Balance and R²D Closure Reciprocity Are Related but Not Identical

Part I defines a directional occurrence-count efficiency for a completed closure:

\[\eta_{n,a,\mathcal{C}} = \frac{\nu_{n,a,L}}{\nu_{n,a,H}}.\]

Reciprocal closure realization satisfies

\[\eta_{n,a,\mathcal{C}} = 1.\]

Chemical detailed balance, however, is usually written for opposed transitions or elementary reaction channels. These are not automatically the same object.

For a minimal two-state chemical closure in which the forward and reverse reaction events are themselves the two complete opposed legs, the identification may be direct:

\[\nu_{H} = N_{+}, \nu_{L} = N_{-}.\]Then equilibrium gives

\[\eta_{\mathcal{C}} = 1.\]

But for a multistep catalytic or biochemical cycle, the complete path counts

\[\nu_{H},\nu_{L}\]

must not be replaced by sums or ratios of individual edge counts without preserving the path structure. Part I explicitly defines these as counts of complete oriented legs, not sums of constituent transition counts. Thus, edgewise detailed balance and closure-level occurrence reciprocity are closely related but logically distinct conditions.

9.11 Directional Bias Is Not Yet Irreversibility

This distinction also prevents another common compression. Away from equilibrium,

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} \neq 1.\]

This establishes a directional kinetic bias. But Part I explicitly states that asymmetric enclosing organization does not mathematically require nonreciprocal occurrence realization around a completed closure.

A closure may possess

\[\Delta P_{\mathcal{C}} \neq 0\]while nevertheless satisfying

\[\eta_{\mathcal{C}} = 1.\]Or it may satisfy

\[\Delta P_{\mathcal{C}} \neq 0\]and

\[0 < \eta_{\mathcal{C}} < 1.\]

Therefore, chemical direction is not the same as R²D irreversibility. Chemical direction answers which reaction realization is favored. R²D irreversibility asks whether a completed closure realizes its opposed legs reciprocally.

Chapter 6 mapped the latter onto entropy production and background. The two relations should remain separate.

9.12 Mass Action Is the Chemical Version of the H-Theorem Problem

The causal issue raised by the H-theorem now becomes even more transparent. Boltzmann kinetics uses local collisions to realize an already statistically organized ensemble:

\[\text{statistical~organization} \rightarrow \text{collision~realization}\text{.}\]

Chemical kinetics uses local reaction events to realize an already thermodynamically organized chemical state:

\[\text{chemical~organization} \rightarrow \text{reaction~realization}\text{.}\]

Mass action therefore does not need to be rejected. It supplies an excellent quantitative realization law. The issue is causal attribution. The kinetic expression

\[J_{+} = k_{+}\prod_{j}a_{j}^{\alpha_{j}}\]

accurately counts forward realization frequency. But the direction

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}}\]

is already constrained by

\[\frac{K_{eq}}{Q}.\]And Chapter 8 showed that

\[- \ln Q = \Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ}.\]

Thus, the local mass-action realization inherits the directional relation from the enclosing chemical state.

9.13 The Strongest Chemical Bridge

The central result of Chapters 1, 8, and 9 can now be written in one chain:

\[\frac{\Theta_{k}}{\Theta_{i}} = \frac{K_{eq}}{Q} = \exp\left( - \frac{\Delta_{r}G}{RT} \right) = \frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}}.\]

Each term belongs to a different scientific language.

\[\frac{\Theta_{k}}{\Theta_{i}}\]is the primitive compatible-count ratio.

\[\frac{K_{eq}}{Q}\]is the chemical-state ratio.

\[- \frac{\Delta_{r}G}{RT}\]is its logarithmic energetic read.

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}}\]is its kinetic realization ratio.

The directional organization does not first appear at the final term. The final term realizes a relation already present in the first. This is the chemical version of the R²D causal architecture:

\[\text{possible~structure} \rightarrow \text{thermodynamic~direction} \rightarrow \text{kinetic~realization}\text{.}\]

9.14 Physical Rate Is a Read of Closure Recurrence

The forward-to-reverse flux ratio does not require a primitive theory of physical time because a common observation interval cancels. Absolute closure recurrence does. If N_Cₙ completed closures are read during a physical interval Δt, define

\[r_{n} = \frac{N_{C,n}}{\Delta t}\]

This physical rate is not 1/ν and not 1/τ. The primitive realization domain defining occurrence count need not be a time interval, and τ=lnν remains a logarithmic occurrence coordinate. Physical rate is a later read of raw recurrence count relative to a physical clock.

\[r_{n} \neq \frac{1}{\nu_{n}} and r_{n} \neq \frac{1}{\tau_{n}}\]

The important consequence is that closure recurrence can vary while native multiplicity remains fixed. The physical rate therefore reads realized recurrence, not a constant of the multiplicity landscape.

9.15 Rate Is Defined Before Activation Barriers

A chemical rate does not require an activation barrier for its definition. For a completed local realization \(\mathcal{C}_{n}\), let

\[N_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\]

be the number of completed recurrences within a physical observation interval \(\Delta t\). The physical recurrence rate is

\[r_{n} = \frac{N_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}}{\Delta t}.\]

This quantity is experimentally defined without introducing an activation energy, transition state, or reaction coordinate. Temperature, pressure, chemical activity, constraint, catalysts, and other enclosing conditions may change the measured recurrence:

\[r_{n} = r_{n}\left( T,p,P_{n},C_{n + 1},\ldots \right).\]

The native state architecture may remain unchanged while the recurrence rate changes.

Arrhenius and Eyring relations provide successful thermodynamic representations of this dependence. They assign quantities such as

\[E_{a}\]and

\[\Delta G^{\ddagger}\]

to the observed variation of recurrence with thermodynamic conditions.

Within R²D, these quantities are therefore not required to define rate. They are later physical coordinates used to represent the dependence of rate on enclosing conditions. Thus,

\[\text{recurrence} \rightarrow \text{physical~rate} \rightarrow \text{activation~representation}\]Not

\[\text{activation~barrier} \rightarrow \text{primitive~occurrence}\text{.}\]

9.16 Reaction Coordinates Are Continuous Projections of Discrete Realization

R²D requires no continuously traversed reaction pathway. A boundary defines distinguishable states and distinguishable occurrences between them. A chemical realization may therefore be represented as

\[i \rightarrow j\]

or as a sequence of distinguishable state changes,

\[i \rightarrow j \rightarrow k \rightarrow \cdots\text{ }.\]

The recurrence of such realizations can be counted directly. A conventional reaction coordinate subsequently places these distinctions along a continuous coordinate and assigns quantities such as activation energy or transition-state free energy to that representation. Thus,

\[\text{discrete~state~distinctions} \rightarrow \text{counted~recurrence} \rightarrow \text{continuous~reaction-coordinate~representation}.\]

The continuous path is not required for the recurrence to exist and is not a primitive R²D object.

This gives a clean interpretation of the word kinetics:

\[\text{Kinetics~is~the~empirical~structure~of~recurrence}\text{.}\]

Arrhenius, Eyring, transition-state theory, diffusion coefficients, catalytic rate constants, and Hill's \(b_{H}\) are then different physical coordinate systems used to describe how recurrence changes under enclosing conditions.

9.17 Coupled Reactions Expose the Boundary Problem

The next step follows almost immediately. Consider a reaction

\[A \rightarrow B\]

that is locally unfavorable:

\[\Delta_{r}G_{1} > 0.\]

If it is coupled to another reaction

\[C \rightarrow D\]

with sufficiently favorable Gibbs change

\[\Delta_{r}G_{2} < 0,\]

the combined process may satisfy

\[\Delta_{r}G_{tot} = \Delta_{r}G_{1} + \Delta_{r}G_{2} < 0.\]

The local reaction has not somehow violated thermodynamics. The relevant thermodynamic boundary has changed. The coupled transformation defines a larger chemical relation.

This as the chemical analogue of the enclosing-reversal theorem. A locally unfavorable process can proceed when embedded within a larger favorable relation. Thus, local chemical relation favors one direction. Enclosing coupled relation favors another.

The local reaction then realizes one branch of the larger relation. This is the natural bridge from ordinary chemical kinetics to biological free-energy transduction.

9.18 ATP Does Not “Push” a Reaction

This point becomes especially important in biology. Statements such as “ATP drives the reaction” are operationally useful. R²D asks what the phrase means structurally.

ATP hydrolysis participates in a larger coupled chemical state whose complete Gibbs relation may favor a transformation that is unfavorable when considered in isolation. The relevant direction is therefore defined by the coupled enclosing chemical boundary. The subordinate reaction step realizes that direction.

9.19 What Has Been Established

Chemical kinetics now occupies a precise position within the Part II architecture.

Thermodynamic direction is

\[\Delta G^{pre} = - \frac{\Delta_{r}G}{RT}.\]

Chemical activity changes that direction through

\[\Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ} = - \ln Q.\]

For a thermodynamically consistent elementary reversible mass-action step,

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} = \frac{K_{eq}}{Q}\]

Therefore,

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} = \frac{\Theta_{k}}{\Theta_{i}} = \exp\left( - \frac{\Delta_{r}G}{RT} \right).\]

The ratio of forward and reverse realization is therefore the kinetic read of a direction already defined by enclosing compatible count. Absolute closure recurrence requires a physical time read and is jointly conditioned by local realization geometry and the enclosing thermodynamic relation:

\[J_{\pm} = \frac{N_{\pm}}{\Delta t}.\]

Thus, thermodynamics determines directional ratio, while the absolute recurrence scale is jointly conditioned by local realization geometry and the enclosing thermodynamic relation. At equilibrium, direction can vanish while forward and reverse realizations remain nonzero.

\[\Delta_{r}G = 0\]and

\[J_{+} = J_{-},\]

while both one-way fluxes may remain nonzero. Mechanism persists. Direction vanishes. The H-theorem and chemical kinetics therefore resolve into the same causal architecture:

\[\text{enclosing~statistical~relation} \rightarrow \text{local~stochastic~realization}\text{.}\]

The remaining chemical problem is coupling. A reaction may be unfavorable when isolated and favorable when embedded within a larger chemical relation.

The next chapter therefore asks how coupled reactions, ATP, ion gradients, proton-motive force, and biological free-energy transduction realize the same enclosing-reversal architecture already proved abstractly in Part I.

Chapter 10 — Coupled Reactions and Biological Free-Energy Transduction

10.1 From Passive Catalysis to Coupling

Chapter 9 separated chemical direction from chemical realization. For a thermodynamically consistent reversible reaction,

\[\frac{J_{+}}{J_{-}} = \frac{K_{eq}}{Q} = \exp\left( - \frac{\Delta_{r}G}{RT} \right).\]

The reaction direction is therefore defined by the enclosing chemical state. The molecular mechanism determines the available local realization geometry, while the enclosing thermodynamic relation and that geometry together determine how rapidly the direction is realized.

\[E \rightarrow ES \rightarrow EP \rightarrow E,\]

or some more complicated conformational closure. It can greatly change the rates of forward and reverse realization without supplying the thermodynamic direction of the reaction.

In the language of Part I, the enzyme is therefore a passive thermal turbine: a local closure through which an enclosing occurrence is realized, but which does not generate the directional relation under which it operates. Part I defines turbines precisely in this causal sense.

Coupled biological systems introduce one additional feature. The protein closure participates simultaneously in more than one thermodynamic relation. A chemical transformation can thereby become compatible with a larger transformation whose direction differs from that of the isolated local reaction. This the problem of biological free-energy transduction.

10.2 An Isolated Reaction and a Coupled Reaction Are Different Thermodynamic Boundaries

Consider a reaction

\[X \rightleftharpoons Y.\]

Suppose that, under the specified chemical conditions,

\[\Delta_{r}G_{1} > 0.\]

The forward reaction is thermodynamically unfavorable. The R²D directional coordinate is

\[\Delta G_{1}^{pre} = - \frac{\Delta_{r}G_{1}}{RT},\]

so

\[\Delta G_{1}^{pre} < 0.\]Now consider a second reaction,

\[A \rightleftharpoons B,\]with

\[\Delta_{r}G_{2} < 0.\]

If the two reactions are physically coupled so that the relevant thermodynamic transformation is

\[X + A \rightarrow Y + B,\]conventional thermodynamics gives

\[\Delta_{r}G_{coup} = \Delta_{r}G_{1} + \Delta_{r}G_{2}.\]

This additive Gibbs relation is standard thermodynamics. The R²D interpretation follows by applying the Gibbs mapping already established in Chapter 1:

\[\Delta G_{coup}^{pre} = - \frac{\Delta_{r}G_{1} + \Delta_{r}G_{2}}{RT}.\]

At common temperature,

\[\Delta G_{coup}^{pre} = \Delta G_{1}^{pre} + \Delta G_{2}^{pre}.\]

If

\[\mid \Delta_{r}G_{2} \mid > \Delta_{r}G_{1},\]

then

\[\Delta_{r}G_{coup} < 0\]

even though

\[\Delta_{r}G_{1} > 0.\]

Therefore,

\[\Delta G_{1}^{pre} < 0 \text{while} \Delta G_{coup}^{pre} > 0.\]

The local reaction and the coupled reaction therefore possess opposite directional reads.

10.3 Coupling Is the Chemical Form of Enclosing Reversal

This is structurally the same result demonstrated abstractly by the enclosing-reversal theorem. The local relation can favor one direction while the larger compatibility relation favors the other. The relevant architecture is

\[\text{local~chemical~relation} \subset \text{coupled~enclosing~chemical~relation}\text{.}\]

The coupled transformation does not make

\[\Delta_{r}G_{1}\]

magically negative. The isolated reaction remains unfavorable when considered on its own boundary. What changes is which thermodynamic boundary defines the realized transformation.

Thus, an unfavorable local reaction need not be unfavorable when embedded in a larger coupled relation. This is an important distinction. The larger relation does not overwrite the local thermodynamic state. It determines which local realization is compatible with the larger occurrence.

10.4 Coupling Is Not Primitive Energy Transfer Between Reactions

The conventional phrase “reaction 2 supplies energy to reaction 1” is operationally useful. But it can suggest a mechanical picture in which one reaction generates a quantity that is subsequently transported into another reaction. R²D does not describe that ontology.

The coupled system has a single enclosing thermodynamic relation:

\[\Delta_{r}G_{coup} = \Delta_{r}G_{1} + \Delta_{r}G_{2}.\]

The local transformation

\[X \rightarrow Y\]

is realized as one branch of that larger compatible relation. Thus, the R²D architecture is that a coupled enclosing relation creates a compatible local reaction realization. Not

\[\text{reaction~2} \longrightarrow \text{packet~of~energy} \longrightarrow \text{reaction~1}.\]

This mirrors Part I's distinction between causal realization and recursive carry: local realizations do not accumulate upward to manufacture the enclosing occurrence. The enclosing relation defines which subordinate closures are compatible with it.

10.5 The Multiplicative Count Structure of Coupling

The same result can be written entirely in count form. For the first reaction,

\[\Delta G_{1}^{pre} = \ln\left( \frac{\Theta_{1, +}}{\Theta_{1, -}} \right).\]For the second,

\[\Delta G_{2}^{pre} = \ln\left( \frac{\Theta_{2, +}}{\Theta_{2, -}} \right).\]

For a coupled relation in which the compatible multiplicities compose multiplicatively,

\[\Delta G_{coup}^{pre} = \Delta G_{1}^{pre} + \Delta G_{2}^{pre}.\]Therefore,

\[\Delta G_{coup}^{pre} = \ln\left\lbrack \frac{\Theta_{1, +}}{\Theta_{1, -}}\frac{\Theta_{2, +}}{\Theta_{2, -}} \right\rbrack.\]Exponentiating,

\[\frac{\Theta_{coup, +}}{\Theta_{coup, -}} = \frac{\Theta_{1, +}}{\Theta_{1, -}}\frac{\Theta_{2, +}}{\Theta_{2, -}}.\]

This is the primitive reason logarithmic free-energy coordinates add when compatible possibilities multiply. Part I already establishes logarithmic additivity under compatible multiplicative composition. Coupled thermodynamics is therefore another physical reading of the same underlying count operation.

10.6 ATP Coupling

ATP provides the most familiar biological example. For ATP hydrolysis, conventional chemical thermodynamics writes

\[ATP + H_{2}O \rightleftharpoons ADP + P_{i},\]with

\[\Delta_{r}G_{ATP} = \Delta_{r}G_{ATP}^{\circ} + RT\ln Q_{ATP}.\]

Under the usual activity convention,

\[Q_{ATP} \propto \frac{a_{ADP}a_{P_{i}}}{a_{ATP}}.\]


The precise standard-state expression depends on the biochemical convention being used, but the structural point does not. The direction of ATP hydrolysis depends on the maintained chemical activity relation. ATP therefore should not be described in R²D as simply “containing energy.” The relevant thermodynamic object is the chemical relation among

\[ATP, ADP, P_{i},\]

and the surrounding chemical state.

Chapter 8 established that the activity-dependent Gibbs contribution maps onto enclosing possibility:

\[\Delta P - \Delta P^{\circ} = - \ln Q.\]

Thus, the ATP relation is itself an enclosing chemical asymmetry.

10.7 ATP Does Not Push the Coupled Reaction

Suppose a local transformation has

\[\Delta_{r}G_{local} > 0.\]

Coupling it to ATP hydrolysis gives

\[\Delta_{r}G_{tot} = \Delta_{r}G_{local} + \Delta_{r}G_{ATP}.\]

If

\[\Delta_{r}G_{tot} < 0,\]

then the coupled transformation is thermodynamically favored.

ATP does not push the local reaction. The ATP-containing chemical boundary defines a larger favorable relation under which the local reaction is realized. The local process remains locally what it is. Its directional significance changes because the count boundary has expanded.

10.8 An Ordinary Enzyme and a Coupled Enzyme Play Different Roles

This gives a useful distinction between two protein architectures. An ordinary catalytic enzyme can be represented as

\[\text{one~enclosing~chemical~relation} \longrightarrow \text{one~protein~closure}\]

Its principal effect is kinetic. It changes the local realization geometry and therefore the recurrence rates without supplying the thermodynamic direction.

A coupled enzyme participates in a larger relation:

\[\text{chemical~relation~1} + \text{chemical~relation~2} \longrightarrow \text{one~coupled~protein~closure}\]

The protein thereby makes realizations of the two relations conditionally linked. The protein still does not create either thermodynamic gradient. Its additional function is to constrain which combinations of local transitions can occur as one completed closure.

Thus, a coupled protein is still a passive turbine with respect to the origin of direction. But it is a more elaborate turbine with respect to how enclosing direction is partitioned among local realizations.

10.9 Coupling Is a Compatibility Constraint

If two reactions occur independently, realization of one need not imply realization of the other. Coupling changes that. A protein architecture can make one transition conditional on another.

Schematically, \(\chi_{1}\) and \(\chi_{2}\) cease to be independently realizable transitions/closures and instead become conditionally-linked parts of a common allowed closure \(\mathcal{C}_{coup}.\)

The coupling mechanism therefore changes the compatibility structure. It determines which combinations of subordinate realizations belong to the larger occurrence. In R²D language, coupling does not create possibility; coupling constraints how possibility can be jointly realized.

That is why structural coupling by a protein can convert a large enclosing chemical relation into a specific local biological outcome.

10.10 Ion Gradients Extend Chemical Coupling Beyond Reactions

The same architecture appears in membrane transport. The following equation is conventional electrochemical thermodynamics rather than a result derived in the attached R²D sources.

For an ion of charge \(z\), transfer between two environments has an electrochemical potential difference of the form

\[\Delta\widetilde{\mu} = RT\ln\left( \frac{a_{2}}{a_{1}} \right) + zF\Delta\psi.\]

Thus, an ion gradient contains both a chemical activity relation and, for charged species, an electrical contribution. A coupled transporter can link this relation to another transformation.

Schematically,

\[\Delta_{r}G_{chem} + n\Delta{\widetilde{\mu}}_{ion} = \Delta G_{coup}\]

A transformation that is unfavorable by itself may therefore occur as one branch of a larger favorable electrochemical relation.

10.11 Proton Gradients Create Another Enclosing Level

Proton gradients are especially important because they make the recursive hierarchy visible. At one level, a protein catalytic closure may be organized by a nucleotide relation. At a larger level, maintenance of the nucleotide relation may depend on proton or metabolic relations. Part I explicitly describes this hierarchy:

\[\text{protein~structural~asymmetry} \leftarrow \text{nucleotide~relation} \leftarrow \text{proton/metabolic~relation} \leftarrow \text{larger~environmental~relation}.\]

Part I states that protein asymmetry depends on an enclosing nucleotide relation, nucleotide asymmetry on enclosing proton and metabolic relations, and those in turn on still larger biological and environmental structures. The causal meaning is not that one unchanged quantity is handed downward through the hierarchy. Each boundary possesses its own possibility, constraint, and asymmetry. What persists is the recursive organization:

\[\text{l}\text{arger~enclosing~relation} \longrightarrow \text{subordinate~directional~realization}\]

10.12 Biological Free-Energy Transduction Is Therefore Recursive Coupling

Biological energy transduction is usually represented as a chain:

\[\text{fuel} \rightarrow \text{ATP} \rightarrow \text{protein} \rightarrow \text{work}.\]

This notation can be useful as bookkeeping. R²D assigns it a different causal interpretation.

Each arrow represents a change in count boundary. A metabolic chemical relation defines compatibility for an enclosing nucleotide relation. The nucleotide relation defines compatibility for protein conformational closure. Protein closure becomes a local realization within a still larger cellular or mechanical boundary. Thus, biological transduction is not transport of one persistent energy object through scales. It is recursive realization of different boundary-specific thermodynamic relations.

This is exactly the kind of hierarchy Part I intends when it says that the subordinate structure answers how, whereas the enclosing structure answers why.

10.13 Molecular Motors Are Coupled Thermal Turbines

A molecular motor differs from a simple enzyme because one of the coupled relations becomes readable as a different kind of distinguishability. A catalytic enzyme may primarily couple chemical state to chemical state. A molecular motor can couple chemical state to mechanical displacement.

Schematically,

\[\text{chemical~asymmetry} \longrightarrow \text{protein~closure} \longrightarrow \text{mechanical~distinguishability}\]

The protein is again the local turbine. The enclosing chemical relation supplies direction.

The protein closure determines how that direction is realized. The larger boundary reads part of that realization through displacement, force, or another productive coordinate.

This connects directly to Chapter 7, where productive enclosing distinguishability was provisionally mapped onto external work through

\[C_{n + 1}^{out}\lambda_{n + 1}.\]

Thus, a molecular motor provides a natural thermodynamic realization of the R²D distinction between local retained closure and externally readable productive output.

10.14 The Motor Does Not Create the Chemical Gradient

The turbine language prevents a common causal inversion. The motor protein does not create

\[\Delta\mu_{ATP}\]

or any other enclosing chemical potential difference under which it operates. Just as a wind turbine does not create the atmospheric pressure difference driving the wind, a molecular turbine does not create the chemical relation organizing its closure. Its role is local realization.

Part I states exactly this principle: a turbine is not a subordinate cause that drives the larger system; it is a local realization of an enclosing occurrence. Thus, motor activity is not the origin or motor direction.

A motor can stop if its enclosing chemical relation vanishes. Its structural mechanism may remain intact. Again, mechanism persists while direction can disappear.

10.15 Muscle Makes the Recursive Hierarchy Macroscopic

Muscle provides a particularly readable example because the enclosing realization becomes macroscopic mechanical structure. Protein switching is required. ATP chemistry is required. Metabolic support is required. But Part I explicitly rejects the idea that the individual protein-switch transition defines the contractile state. It describes protein switching as the subordinate realization of a larger muscle multiplicity structure.

The hierarchy can therefore be written schematically as

\[\text{metabolic/environmental~relation} \longrightarrow \text{nucleotide~relation} \longrightarrow \text{myosin~closure} \longrightarrow \text{muscle~occupancy} \longrightarrow \text{mechanical~}\text{output.}\]

No single step contains the whole causal relation. The myosin mechanism answers how the protein-scale realization occurs. The ATP relation answers why particular protein states are supported. The muscle boundary determines which collective protein realizations constitute contraction. The external mechanical boundary determines how the resulting distinguishability is read as load, shortening, and work.

10.16 Coupling Can Reverse Local Direction

This returns us to the central mathematical result. For an isolated local relation,

\[\Delta G_{local}^{pre} < 0\]

may oppose the transition. For the larger coupled relation,

\[\Delta G_{coup}^{pre} > 0.\]

The coupled protein closure can therefore realize the local transition in the direction opposite to that favored when the local reaction is considered alone. This does not violate the local Gibbs relation. It changes the relevant thermodynamic boundary. Thus, coupling is not an exception to thermodynamic direction; it reveals that thermodynamic direction is boundary relative.

10.17 Coupling Does Not Necessarily Mean Irreversibility

One distinction from Chapters 5 and 6 must be preserved. A coupled process can possess a nonzero thermodynamic gradient without necessarily producing irreversible background in every idealized realization. Directional asymmetry is

\[\Delta G^{pre} \neq 0.\]

Irreversible closure is separately associated with nonreciprocal occurrence realization:

\[L_{\tau,\mathcal{C}} > 0.\]

Part I explicitly separates enclosing asymmetry from occurrence-count reciprocity. A closure can be asymmetrically organized while its opposed occurrence realization remains reciprocal. Therefore, coupling is not irreversibility. Likewise, productive output is not entropy production.

A reversible coupled turbine is conceptually possible. Real biological turbines will generally operate with finite dissipation, but the existence of coupling itself does not define that dissipation.

10.18 Productive and Dissipative Realization Reappear

Part I decomposes enclosing constraint into

\[C_{n + 1} = C_{n + 1}^{out} + C_{n + 1}^{\Phi}.\]

The retaining/output component produces local retention and is read at the enclosing boundary as distinguishable output:

\[C_{n + 1}^{out}\lambda_{n + 1},\]

while the background-associated component does not enter local retention and is read separately in the enclosing realization as background-associated constraint.

A coupled biological turbine therefore raises a new question. Given an enclosing chemical relation, how much of its realization remains readable as productive distinguishability, and how much is associated with irreversible background? This question is not answered by Gibbs direction alone. It requires the full closure partition.

10.19 From Enzyme to Motor to Organism

The biological sequence can now be organized by increasing enclosing structure. A simple enzyme is a passive chemical turbine:

\[\text{chemical~gradient} \rightarrow \text{catalytic~closure}.\]

A coupled enzyme links two thermodynamic relations:

\[\text{gradient~1} + \text{gradient~2} \rightarrow \text{coupled~closure}.\]

A molecular motor couples chemical closure to mechanical distinguishability:

\[\text{chemical~relation} \rightarrow \text{protein~closure} \rightarrow \text{mechanical~output}.\]

A metabolic system maintains the nucleotide and electrochemical relations under which these proteins operate. And Part I extends the causal hierarchy beyond metabolism to environmental structure and ultimately solar input.

Thus, biology does not introduce a fundamentally new thermodynamic principle. It increases the depth of recursive coupling.

10.20 What Has Been Established

The thermodynamic result is straightforward:

\[\Delta_{r}G_{coup} = \sum_{j}\Delta_{r}G_{j}.\]

At common \(T\), the R²D directional reads therefore add:

\[\Delta G_{coup}^{pre} = \sum_{j}\Delta G_{j}^{pre}\]

A locally unfavorable reaction may therefore be embedded within a larger favorable relation:

\[\Delta_{r}G_{local} > 0\]while

\[\Delta_{r}G_{coup} < 0.\]

The R²D interpretation is that local direction is not the enclosing coupled direction. Protein enzymes provide the local closure through which these relations are realized. Simple enzymes are passive thermal turbines. Coupled enzymes make distinct thermodynamic relations conditionally realizable through one closure. Molecular motors extend the same architecture into mechanical distinguishability. And biology recursively embeds these local turbines within nucleotide, electrochemical, metabolic, organismal, and environmental relations.

The central causal architecture is:

\[\text{enclosing~thermodynamic~relation} \longrightarrow \text{protein~closure} \longrightarrow \text{larger-scale~realization}\]

This brings Part II to the next question. Once a thermal turbine is embedded within an enclosing gradient, what determines the partition between retained local realization, productive external output, and irreversible background?

Chapter 11 — The Thermodynamic Realization of the R²D Law: Muscle, Hill, Output, and Background

11.1 The R²D Law Now Has a Thermodynamic Rate Read

Part I defines a turbine as a local closure through which an enclosing occurrence is realized. The local closure does not generate the enclosing occurrence. Rather, the enclosing occurrence defines the compatible local realization through which it becomes readable. The revised R²D law makes the recurrence relation between these two boundary readings explicit.

For a representative closure \(\mathcal{C}_{n,a}\) at \(B_{n}\), let

\[\Delta A_{n,a,\mathcal{C}}\]

be the residual enclosing-defined asymmetry around the completed local closure.

At \(B_{n + 1}\), the same recursively organized realization is read through enclosing distinguishability

\[\lambda_{n + 1}\]

and enclosing constraint

\[C_{n + 1}.\]

Let

\[N_{\mathcal{D}_{R}}\left( \mathcal{C}_{n,a} \right)\]

count completed local closure recurrences and

\[N_{\mathcal{D}_{R}}\left( \chi_{n + 1} \right)\]

count completed enclosing occurrences over one common recurrence-comparison domain \(\mathcal{D}_{R}\). Part I postulates

\[\Delta A_{n,a,\mathcal{C}}N_{\mathcal{D}_{R}}\left( \mathcal{C}_{n,a} \right) = C_{n + 1}\lambda_{n + 1}N_{\mathcal{D}_{R}}\left( \chi_{n + 1} \right).\]

Equivalently, defining

\[\rho_{n + 1 \mid n,a} = \frac{N_{\mathcal{D}_{R}}\left( \chi_{n + 1} \right)}{N_{\mathcal{D}_{R}}\left( \mathcal{C}_{n,a} \right)},\]the R²D law becomes

\[\Delta A_{n,a,\mathcal{C}} = C_{n + 1}\lambda_{n + 1}\rho_{n + 1 \mid n,a}.\]

This relation remains dimensionless. Part I assigns no elapsed time to either recurrence count and explicitly leaves a physical-rate interpretation to later domain-specific mapping. Thermodynamics supplies that mapping. When both recurrence counts are observed over the same physical interval \(\Delta t\),

\[r_{n} = \frac{N_{\mathcal{D}_{R}}\left( \mathcal{C}_{n} \right)}{\Delta t}\]and

\[r_{n + 1} = \frac{N_{\mathcal{D}_{R}}\left( \chi_{n + 1} \right)}{\Delta t}.\]Therefore,

\[\rho_{n + 1 \mid n} = \frac{r_{n + 1}}{r_{n}}.\]

The R²D law acquires the physical-rate read

\[\Delta A_{n}r_{n} = C_{n + 1}\lambda_{n + 1}r_{n + 1}\]

This is the relation that Chapter 7 left to be physically calibrated. Muscle supplies that calibration.

11.2 Local Asymmetry and Enclosing Output Are Different Boundary Reads

The left and right sides of the R²D law must retain their boundary-specific meanings. Locally,

\[A_{n} = P_{n} - R_{n}\]

Possibility \(P_{n}\) is defined by compatible enclosing multiplicity. Retention is

\[R_{n} = C_{n + 1}^{out}\lambda_{n}.\]

Thus \(A_{n}\) is the residual enclosing-defined asymmetry remaining after local retention. At the enclosing boundary, the same recursively organized realization is read instead through

\[C_{n + 1}\lambda_{n + 1}.\]

The R²D law does not assert

\[R_{n} = C_{n + 1}\lambda_{n + 1}.\]

Nor does it assert that \(A_{n}\) is a substance exported upward. It says that local recurrence-weighted asymmetry and enclosing recurrence-weighted constrained distinguishability are two boundary-specific reads of one recursive realization. Part I explicitly preserves that causal distinction: the local closure does not generate the enclosing occurrence.

The thermodynamic problem is therefore not one of transporting \(A\) from \(B_{n}\) to \(B_{n + 1}\). It is to determine the physical coordinates in which the two sides become jointly readable.

11.3 The Enclosing Constraint Has Retaining/Output and Background-Associated Components

Part I decomposes the enclosing constraint as

\[C_{n + 1} = C_{n + 1}^{out} + C_{n + 1}^{\Phi}\]

The retaining/output component

\[C_{n + 1}^{out}\]

produces local retention and remains readable at the enclosing boundary as distinguishable output. The background-associated component

\[C_{n + 1}^{\Phi}\]

does not enter the local retention term; it appears separately in the enclosing realization.

The two components therefore occupy different logical positions. At the subordinate boundary, only the retaining/output component enters retention. At the enclosing boundary, the complete constraint is read as the sum of retaining/output and background-associated contributions.

The two components are not separate transitions and not separate microscopic pathways. They partition the read of the same enclosing constrained realization.

The rate-read R²D law therefore becomes

\[\Delta A_{n}r_{n} = \left( C_{n + 1}^{out} + C_{n + 1}^{\Phi} \right)\lambda_{n + 1}r_{n + 1}\]or

\[\Delta A_{n}r_{n} = C_{n + 1}^{out}\lambda_{n + 1}r_{n + 1} + C_{n + 1}^{\Phi}\lambda_{n + 1}r_{n + 1}.\]

This is the dimensionless rate partition to be calibrated thermodynamically.

11.4 The Energetic Read

Chapter 7 introduced energetic reads of the primitive quantities. For an isothermal realization,

\[\mathcal{P}_{n} = k_{B}T\text{ }P_{n},\]

\[{\mathcal{R}_{n} = k_{B}T\text{ }R_{n}, }\]and

\[\mathcal{A}_{n} = k_{B}T\text{ }A_{n}.\]Thus,

\[\mathcal{A}_{n} = \mathcal{P}_{n} - \mathcal{R}_{n}.\]

Under a common thermodynamic calibration, the rate-read R2D law becomes

\[\mathcal{A}_{n}r_{n} = {\dot{\mathcal{E}}}_{n + 1}^{out} + {\dot{\mathcal{E}}}_{n + 1}^{\Phi}.\]

The first term is the energetic rate retained as productive enclosing output. The second is the energetic rate associated with background-associated enclosing realization. This is the general thermodynamic architecture. Muscle makes every term experimentally readable.

11.5 Muscle Is the Empirical Calibration System

Muscle is unusually useful because both sides of the recursive relation can be measured within one functioning thermodynamic system. At the local boundary, the myosin ensemble behaves as a binary system of protein switches. Its native multiplicity landscape has the combinatorial form

\[W_{i} = C(N_{sw}, i)\]

The biochemical cycle changes occupancy within this landscape and, under changed enclosing conditions, can also change the frequency with which the local closure recurs.

The earlier thermal-turbine formulation of muscle identifies a stored directional energetic difference, a stall force, a molecular step \(d\), a cycling rate \(v\), external work \(FV\), and shortening heat. It explicitly gives stall when caloric and mechanical potentials balance and gives Hill's force-velocity relation from the resulting power balance.

This makes muscle a direct empirical candidate for the physical realization of the R²D law.

11.6 Possibility, Retention, and Asymmetry in Muscle

Let

\[F_{o}\]

denote maximum isometric, or stall, force. Let

\[d\]

be the locally readable myosin mechanical interval. The muscle energetic read of possibility is

\(\mathcal{P}_{n} = F_{o}d\).

Under load \(F\), the energetic read of local retention is

\[\mathcal{R}_{n} = Fd.\]Therefore,

\[\mathcal{A}_{n} = \mathcal{P}_{n} - \mathcal{R}_{n} = \left( F_{o} - F \right)d.\]The R²D sequence

\[P \rightarrow R \rightarrow A\]therefore acquires the muscle read

\[F_{o}d \rightarrow Fd \rightarrow \left( F_{o} - F \right)d.\]

This is not a microscopic power-stroke equation. It is the energetic relation of the ensemble landscape within which molecular switching is realized.

11.7 Stall Is \(P = R\)

The stall condition follows directly. Complete retention satisfies

\[A_{n} = 0.\]Equivalently,

\[P_{n} = R_{n}.\]Energetically,

\[\mathcal{P}_{n} = \mathcal{R}_{n}.\]For muscle

\[F_{o}d = Fd,\]so

\[F = F_{o}.\]

The earlier thermal-turbine analysis reaches exactly this physical condition: stall occurs when the driving caloric potential equals mechanical opposition, and for step size \(d\),

\[F_{stall} = \frac{\Delta CP}{d}.\]

It identifies the same condition for thermal ratchets and biological molecular motors. Thus:

\[\text{stall\:\,} \Longleftrightarrow \text{\:\,}P = R\text{\:\,} \Longleftrightarrow \text{\:\,}A = 0.\]

Zero shortening velocity is not the primitive definition of stall. It is the enclosing mechanical consequence. Complete retention gives

\[A_{C} = 0\]

\[C_{n + 1} \lambda_{n + 1} r_{n + 1} = 0\]

For a mechanically constrained motor with nonzero enclosing constraint, the productive mechanical read therefore gives

\[V = 0\]

The causal order is

\[P = R \Rightarrow A = 0 \Rightarrow V = 0\]

This matters experimentally because an isometric muscle can remain chemically active while shortening is zero; the protein-state ensemble can remain active and structurally asymmetric under isometric conditions.

11.8 ATPase Reads Local Closure Recurrence

Let \(N_{sw}\) denote the total realized number of two-state myosin switches defining the ensemble at Bₙ. \(N_{sw}\) is the realized ensemble size underlying the binary multiplicity well; it is not the occupancy of one macrostate.

\[N_{A} + N_{B} = N_{sw}, W_{i} = C(N_{sw}, i)\]

The attached and detached occupancies may redistribute while \(N_{sw}\) and the native binary classification remain fixed. If one ATP hydrolysis is used as the stoichiometric read of one completed local switch cycle, the bulk ATPase event rate \(J_{ATP}\) reads the total number of completed local recurrences:

\[J_{ATP} = N_{sw} r_{n}\]

Thus, the per-switch local recurrence-rate read is

\[r_{n} = \frac{J_{ATP}}{N_{sw}}\]

No additional load-dependent 'cycling occupancy' factor is required. Under a fixed ensemble boundary, force-dependent ATPase therefore means that local closure recurrence changes with enclosing mechanical constraint even though the native multiplicity well remains fixed.

\[\Delta W_{n} = 0, \Delta N_{sw} = 0, r_{n} = r_{n}(C_{n + 1}, P_{n}, \ldots)\]

11.9 Hill's b Is the Local Recurrence-Weighted Distinguishability

Let λₙ have the local mechanical read dₙ. The physical quantity that Hill places on the local side of the force-velocity relation is not recurrence rate alone but local distinguishability multiplied by recurrence rate. Define

\[b_{H} \equiv \lambda_{n} r_{n} \rightarrow d_{n} r_{n}\]

Hill's \(b\) is therefore the local mechanical read of recurrence-weighted distinguishability at Bₙ. It is not a primitive constant of Wₙ. Changing enclosing temperature, pressure, nucleotide state, or mechanical constraint may change rₙ without reconstructing the multiplicity well; λₙ may also acquire a condition-dependent physical read.

Accordingly, Hill constrains the product dₙrₙ. It does not, by itself, require either dₙ or rₙ to remain separately constant.

11.10 Shortening Is the Enclosing Recurrence Read

At the enclosing muscle boundary, the readable mechanical occurrence is shortening. Let λₙ₊₁ be the enclosing displacement per enclosing recurrence and rₙ₊₁ its recurrence-rate read. Then

\[V = \lambda_{n + 1} r_{n + 1}\]

The two sides of the physical R²D mapping therefore contain adjacent recurrence-weighted distinguishabilities: \(b_{H}\) locally and \(V\) enclosingly.

11.11 Productive and Frictional Constraint Reads Are Boundary-Specific

The retaining/output component of enclosing constraint has two boundary-specific physical reads. At the local myosin boundary it produces retention:

\[k_{B}T R_{n} = k_{B}T C_{n + 1}^{out} \lambda_{n} \leftrightarrow F d_{n}\]

At the enclosing mechanical boundary, that same retaining/output component remains readable as productive force, while the background-associated component is read separately as frictional force:

\[k_{B}T C_{n + 1}^{out} \lambda_{n + 1} r_{n + 1} \leftrightarrow FV\]

\[k_{B}T C_{n + 1}^{\Phi} \lambda_{n + 1} r_{n + 1} \leftrightarrow a_{H}V\]

These are product-level, boundary-specific physical reads. R2D does not identify the bare dimensionless constraint with one universal force. Locally, retention is the read of the retaining/output component acting through local distinguishability; enclosingly, the complete constraint is the sum of retaining/output and background-associated contributions acting on enclosing recurrence-weighted distinguishability. The recursive law relates these boundary-specific products.

11.12 Hill Is the Mechanical Calibration of the R²D Law

The energetic read of local muscle asymmetry is

\[k_{B}T A_{n} = (F_{o} - F)d_{n}\]

Multiplying by the local recurrence-rate read gives

\[k_{B}T A_{n} r_{n} = (F_{o} - F)b_{H}\]

The enclosing productive and frictional reads give

\[k_{B}T C_{n + 1} \lambda_{n + 1} r_{n + 1} = (F + a_{H})V\]

Therefore the physical muscle realization of the recurrence-weighted R2D law is

\((F_{o} - F)b_{H} = (F + a_{H})V\).

Here \(F\) is the productive force-coordinate read and \(a_{H}\) is the effective frictional force-coordinate read. The empirical Hill relation therefore calibrates both sides of the R²D law in one jointly readable thermodynamic system.

11.13 The Hill Hyperbola Follows Under a Fixed Thermodynamic Calibration

For a force-velocity curve measured under a specified thermodynamic condition, write

\[(F + a_{H})V = b_{H}(F_{o} - F)\]

If \(b_{H}\) and \(a_{H}\) are constant over that fitted curve, algebra gives

\[(F + a_{H})(V + b_{H}) = b_{H}(F_{o} + a_{H})\]

This is the conventional Hill hyperbola. The constancy is a property of the specified empirical calibration, not a claim that \(b_{H}\) is a universal constant of the native multiplicity landscape. Across changes in temperature, pressure, biochemical state, or other enclosing conditions, b_H may change. Even within a condition where fitted \(b_{H}\) is approximately constant, the equation constrains the product dₙrₙ rather than requiring the local distinguishability and recurrence rate to be separately constant.

11.14 What Hill Calibrates—and What It Does Not

Part I does not derive Fₒ, dₙ, rₙ, a_H, or their condition dependence from primitive combinatorics. Nor does it identify muscle as the unique physical realization of the R2D law. What Part I supplies is the dimensionless recursive relation between local asymmetry, enclosing constraint, and adjacent recurrence. Muscle supplies the empirical reads

\[k_{B}T A_{n} = (F_{o} - F)d_{n}\]

\[b_{H} = d_{n} r_{n}\]

\[V = \lambda_{n + 1} r_{n + 1}\]

\[C^{out} \rightarrow F, C^{\Phi} \rightarrow a_{H} (boundary - specific force - coordinate reads)\]

Under these mappings the R²D relation becomes Hill's equation exactly. Every parameter can been experimentally measured, and the relation holds under many different parameter perturbations. Hill is therefore an empirical thermodynamic calibration of the abstract R²D law, not a first-principles derivation of the muscle parameters.

11.15 The Two Zero-Power Limits Are Now Different for a Clear Reason

Hill's relation contains two familiar limiting conditions. At stall,

\[F = F_{o}.\]

Then

\[\mathcal{A} = 0\]and therefore

\[V = 0.\]External mechanical power vanishes:

\[FV = 0.\]

But it vanishes because there is no residual local asymmetry available for enclosing realization. At zero load,

\[F = 0.\]Then

\[\mathcal{R =}0\]and

\[\mathcal{A = P =}F_{o}d.\]

The local available asymmetry is maximal. Yet productive mechanical power again vanishes:

\[FV = 0.\]

The two conditions therefore have opposite local meanings. At stall:

\[A = 0.\]At zero load:

\[A = P.\]

Thus, zero external power does not identify a unique thermodynamic state. This is why productive output cannot be used to define either stall or thermodynamic direction.

11.16 Shortening Heat Is the Background-Associated Mechanical Read

Hill's relation makes the second output term explicit:

\[{\dot{Q}}_{short} = aF_{o}V.\]

This is the mechanical energetic read of the background-associated constraint:

\[{\dot{Q}}_{short} \longleftrightarrow k_{B}TC^{\Phi}\lambda_{n + 1}r_{n + 1}.\]

This gives \(C^{\Phi}\) a much clearer physical interpretation than the previous manuscript had available. It is not itself heat. It is the constraint component whose realized mechanical work is read as heat when the enclosing directional realization thermalizes. Thus:

\[C^{\Phi} \rightarrow F_{\Phi} \rightarrow F_{\Phi}V \rightarrow {\dot{Q}}_{\Phi}.\]For muscle,

\[F_{\Phi} = aF_{o}.\]

This is a thermodynamic mapping of the R²D constraint partition, not a new primitive definition inserted into Part I.

11.17 Frictional Constraint and Background Increment Remain Distinct

The new muscle mapping does not eliminate the distinction established in Part I. The R²D-law term

\[C^{\Phi}\lambda\]

belongs to the partition of enclosing constrained realization. The Second Law separately defines the enclosing background increment

\[\delta_{\mathcal{C}}\Phi_{n + 1}\]

from local occurrence-count nonreciprocity. Part I explicitly does not postulate

\[C^{\Phi}\lambda = \delta_{\mathcal{C}}\Phi.\]

The muscle mapping instead supplies a possible thermodynamic bridge. If frictional power

\[F_{\Phi}V\]

is irreversibly thermalized at temperature \(T\), then

\[{\dot{S}}_{gen} = \frac{F_{\Phi}V}{T}.\]For muscle,

\[{\dot{S}}_{gen} = \frac{aF_{o}V}{T}.\]

Chapter 6 identifies generated thermodynamic entropy with the R²D background increment through

\[S_{gen} = k_{B}\delta\Phi.\]

Therefore, for this specified isothermal thermalization,

\[\dot{\Phi} = \frac{aF_{o}V}{k_{B}T}.\]

This is a domain-specific thermodynamic relation. It does not establish the primitive identity

\[C^{\Phi}\lambda = \delta\Phi\]

for all R²D realizations.

11.18 Productive Output and Irreversibility Are Still Different Relations

The Hill calibration makes productive and frictional reads visible, but it must not collapse the R²D law into the Second Law. Part I defines irreversibility through opposed-leg occurrence nonreciprocity.

If

\[\nu_{H}\]and

\[\nu_{L}\]

are the complete occurrence counts of the two opposed closure legs, then

\[\eta_{\mathcal{C}} = \frac{\nu_{L}}{\nu_{H}},\]and occurrence-count loss is

\[L_{\tau} = - \ln\eta_{\mathcal{C}}.\]

The Second Law gives

\[\delta_{\mathcal{C}}\Phi_{n + 1} = L_{\tau}.\]

Productive output instead belongs to

\[C^{out}\lambda_{n + 1}r_{n + 1}.\]Thus

\[\text{productive~output} \neq \text{irreversibility}\text{.}\]

Likewise,

\[C^{\Phi} \neq L_{\tau}.\]

The first relation partitions how the enclosing constrained occurrence is physically read. The second measures directional nonreciprocity of realization. These roles remain distinct in the muscle calibration.
